```
18: """Resource: specmgr://qa/schema (Phase 4, Task 4.2).
19: 
20: Reads QA's generated JSON Schema from the packaged data copy
21: (``qa/data/qa_schema.json``, via ``general.tools._packaged_data.read_packaged_text``)
22: rather than ``docs/qa_schema.json`` directly -- the latter is only readable
23: from an editable/source checkout, which would break for a real,
24: non-editable ``pip install``. The packaged copy is kept in sync with
25: ``docs/qa_schema.json`` by a dedicated pre-commit hook/CI step that runs
26: ``specmgr schema --type qa --output-dir src/biz/dfch/specmgr/qa/data``
27: -- the same generator as ``docs/qa_schema.json``, just a second
28: ``--output-dir``, so no bespoke copy logic exists in ``commands/schema.py``.
29: Deliberately does not import ``commands.schema.generate_qa_schema()``
30: (which would leak the ``cli`` extra's ``typer`` dependency into the ``mcp``
31: extra's import graph), nor regenerate the file on the fly -- this is a
32: plain, read-only read of a build-time-guaranteed file. 1:1 port of
33: ``req.resources.req_schema``.
34: 
35: The resource's URI is deliberately unversioned (no ``/v1``) even though the
36: file it reads is a ``qa/models/v1``-derived artifact -- see
37: `.specmgr/feat/feat-6-requirement-artifact/README.md`'s Decisions Made for
38: the original rationale, reused verbatim here.
39: """
40: 
41: from __future__ import annotations
42: 
43: import json
44: from typing import Any
45: 
46: from ...general.tools._packaged_data import read_packaged_text
47: from ...server import mcp
48: 
49: 
50: @mcp.resource(
51:     "specmgr://qa/schema",
52:     name="qa_schema",
53:     title="Question and Answer (QA) JSON Schema",
54:     description=(
55:         "The generated QA JSON Schema (2020-12 dialect), generated by `specmgr schema` and "
56:         "kept current by a pre-commit hook/CI step. Includes a `$comment` schema-layout version "
57:         "marker for detecting a shape change without diffing the whole document."
58:     ),
59:     mime_type="application/json",
60: )
61: def qa_schema() -> dict[str, Any]:
62:     """Return the parsed contents of QA's packaged JSON Schema.
63: 
64:     Reads the packaged copy (``qa/data/qa_schema.json``) fresh
65:     on every call (no in-memory cache, consistent with every other
66:     resource/tool in this codebase) but never regenerates it -- its
67:     presence is guaranteed at build time (real package data, kept in sync
68:     with ``docs/qa_schema.json`` by a dedicated pre-commit hook/CI step),
69:     so a missing or corrupted file is treated as a hard failure rather than
70:     defensively handled.
71: 
72:     Returns
73:     -------
74:     dict[str, Any]
75:         The parsed JSON Schema document (top-level keys include
76:         ``$schema``, ``$comment``, ``$defs``, ``properties``, ...).
77: 
78:     Raises
79:     ------
80:     FileNotFoundError
81:         If the packaged ``qa_schema.json`` is missing.
82:     json.JSONDecodeError
83:         If the packaged file is not valid JSON.
84:     """
85:     result: dict[str, Any] = json.loads(read_packaged_text("qa", "schema", "json"))
86:     return result

(End of file - total 86 lines)
</content>
```

```
71: class TempQaDirTestCase(unittest.TestCase):
72:     """Common fixture: a temp dir set as the docs root via SPECMGR_DOCS_DIR."""
73: 
74:     def setUp(self) -> None:

79: class TestCreateQa(TempQaDirTestCase):
80:     """Tests for the create_qa tool."""
81: 
82:     def test_builds_frontmatter_and_returns_document(self) -> None:
83:         """create_qa must build the entire frontmatter itself (id/type/status/timestamps/version)."""
84:         result = create_qa(_MINIMAL_BODY)
85: 
86:         self.assertIsInstance(result, QaDocument)
87:         self.assertIsNotNone(result.frontmatter.id)
88:         self.assertEqual(result.frontmatter.type, "qa")
89:         self.assertEqual(result.frontmatter.status, "draft")
90:         self.assertIsNotNone(result.frontmatter.created)
91:         self.assertEqual(result.frontmatter.created, result.frontmatter.updated)
92:         self.assertEqual(result.frontmatter.version, CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION)
93:         self.assertEqual(result.body.text, "Some QA Title")
94: 
95:     def test_writes_expected_filename(self) -> None:
96:         """create_qa must write f'qa-{id}-{slug}.md' under the QA base dir."""
97:         result = create_qa(_MINIMAL_BODY)
98: 
99:         expected_path = qa_base_dir() / f"qa-{result.frontmatter.id}-some-qa-title.md"
100:         self.assertTrue(expected_path.exists())
101: 
102:     def test_written_file_round_trips_via_parse_qa(self) -> None:
103:         """The written file must parse back into an equivalent document."""
104:         result = create_qa(_MINIMAL_BODY)
105: 
106:         expected_path = qa_base_dir() / f"qa-{result.frontmatter.id}-some-qa-title.md"
107:         on_disk = parse_qa(expected_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
108: 
109:         self.assertEqual(on_disk.frontmatter.id, result.frontmatter.id)
110:         self.assertEqual(on_disk.frontmatter.status, "draft")
111:         self.assertEqual(on_disk.body.text, "Some QA Title")
112:         self.assertIsNone(on_disk.body.compatibility.items)
113: 
114:     def test_creates_base_dir_if_missing(self) -> None:
115:         """create_qa must create the QA base directory if it does not exist yet."""
116:         self.assertFalse(qa_base_dir().exists())
117: 
118:         create_qa(_MINIMAL_BODY)

120:         self.assertTrue(qa_base_dir().is_dir())
121: 
122:     def test_invalid_content_raises_and_writes_nothing(self) -> None:
123:         """A structurally invalid body must raise AssertionError and write no file at all.
124: 
125:         Unlike `req.tools.create_req` (whose body has a field with a closed-set
126:         validator, e.g. `## Level`), `qa`'s body has no caller-controllable field
127:         that can trigger `pydantic.ValidationError` on its own -- every category
128:         heading is either fixed text (a structural match, not a field value) or
129:         fully optional. So there is only one error channel to exercise here.

131:         with self.assertRaises(AssertionError):
```

```
## Summary Report

**Task 4.1 — Repointed all 10 QA tools at `qa/models/v2/`:**

Files modified (`src/`):
- `qa/tools/create_qa.py`, `update_qa.py`, `set_status_qa.py`, `parse_qa.py`, `get_qa.py`, `validate_qa.py` — import `Qa`/`QaDocument`/`QaFrontmatter`/`parse_qa` from `..models.v2` now; docstring `:class:` refs updated to `v2`.
- `qa/tools/_paths.py`, `_write.py`, `_io.py` — same import/docstring repoint.
- `qa/tools/list_qa.py` — kept `QaSummary` import from `..models.v1` (deliberate, per plan instructions — generic DTO, no `Qa` coupling); only its `parse_qa` docstring cross-reference moved to `v2`.
- `get_qa_example.py`, `get_qa_template.py`, `delete_qa.py` — verified no model imports/docstring references; untouched.
- `create_qa.py`'s `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` import from `...models.md` left untouched, as instructed.

**Task 4.2 — Test rewrite (`tests/qa/tools/`):**

10 of 14 files modified: `test__io.py`, `test__paths.py`, `test__write.py`, `test_create_qa.py`, `test_get_qa.py`, `test_list_qa.py`, `test_parse_qa.py`, `test_set_status_qa.py`, `test_update_qa.py`, `test_validate_qa.py`. 4 files needed no change (verified by reading each): `test__lock.py`, `test_delete_qa.py`, `test_get_qa_example.py`, `test_get_qa_template.py`.

Changes: every body-markdown fixture gained mandatory `## Elicitation Context`; `.items`/v1's `### {heading}`-per-question assertions converted to `.questions`/adjacent-pair shape; `QaDocument` imports moved `v1`→`v2` (confirmed these are genuinely distinct classes, so this was required for `isinstance` correctness, not cosmetic); `test__write.py` kept `QaFrontmatter` pinned to `v1` (deliberately shared) while its `parse_qa` moved to `v2`.

Added explicit ACC-005 tests for each read-path tool:
- `test_parse_qa.py::test_raises_structural_error_for_v1_shaped_document`
- `test_validate_qa.py::test_raises_structural_error_for_v1_shaped_{body_only_content,full_document}`
- `test_get_qa.py::test_read_path_surfaces_structural_error_for_v1_shaped_document` + `test__io.py::TestReadQa.test_raises_structural_error_for_v1_shaped_document` (see Decisions Made note below on why `get_qa` itself needed this at the `_io.read_qa` layer)

**Quality gate — all green:**
```
uv run --frozen ruff format --check        → 830 files already formatted
uv run --frozen ruff check                 → All checks passed!
uv run --frozen vulture src/ whitelist.py --min-confidence 60  → no findings
uv run --frozen python -m unittest discover -v -s tests -t . -p "test_*.py"  → Ran 1322 tests, OK
```
(1317 → 1322 tests: +5 net new tests, offsetting no removals.)

**Untouched, confirmed:** `models/md/`, `qa/models/v1/`, and Phases 1–3's `qa/models/v2/{question_answer,body,document,parser,__init__}.py` — `git status` shows zero changes under any of these paths.

**Not committed:** confirmed — branch is `feat-14`, `git status --short` shows only unstaged modifications, no commits made, no branch changes.

**REQ-005 final wording:** "Repoint every QA MCP tool (...) at `qa/models/v2/`." (dropped the "routed through REQ-004's version gate" clause).

**ACC-005 final wording:** "...operates against v2 documents, and surfaces the same structural `AssertionError`/`pydantic.ValidationError` `Qa.from_text`/`QaFrontmatter.model_validate` raise on their own for a v1-shaped document passed to a read path (`get_qa`/`parse_qa`/`validate_qa`), per REQ-004's revised (Phase 3) no-gate design."

**One design decision worth your attention (documented in README's Decisions Made):** `get_qa`'s id-based lookup (`find_qa_path`) *silently skips* files that fail to parse — pre-existing, unrelated to this cutover. A v1-shaped file therefore can never be *found* by id at all, so `get_qa(id)` on it surfaces `QaNotFoundError`, not the structural parse error. I resolved this by writing the `get_qa` ACC-005 test one layer down against `qa.tools._io.read_qa` (what `get_qa` actually delegates to once a path is resolved) rather than treating it as a blocker. Please review this reasoning — it's the one place I made a judgment call rather than following the instructions literally.

Progress section (Current Status, Recent Updates, Decisions Made, task checkboxes) updated in `.specmgr/feat/feat-14-qa-v2-adjacent-qa/README.md`. Ready for your review before Phase 5.
```

```
"""Resource: specmgr://qa/example (Phase 4, Task 4.2).

Read-only, addressable counterpart of the ``get_qa_example`` tool, mirroring
this repo's existing tool+resource pairs (e.g. ``get_req_example`` /
``specmgr://req/example``) for a host that wants to fetch the example as
context without an explicit tool call. Deliberately does not import from
``qa.tools`` (nor vice versa): both this resource and the ``get_qa_example``
tool import the shared, doc-type-agnostic ``general.tools._packaged_data``
helper directly, so neither sub-package depends on the other just for this
one file read.

The resource's URI is deliberately unversioned (no ``/v1``), matching
``specmgr://qa/schema``'s own precedent.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from ...general.tools._packaged_data import read_packaged_text
from ...server import mcp


@mcp.resource(
    "specmgr://qa/example",
    name="qa_example",
    title="Question and Answer (QA) Example",
    description=(
        "A complete, valid sample QA document as raw markdown -- frontmatter and body -- "
        "exercising every section, for use as a learning example."
    ),
    mime_type="text/markdown",
)
def qa_example() -> str:
    """Return the packaged QA example's full markdown text, verbatim.

    Same packaged-data source and no-cache, hard-failure-on-missing-file
    design as ``qa.tools.get_qa_example.get_qa_example`` -- this is simply
    that same read exposed as an MCP resource instead of a ``@mcp.tool()``.

    Returns
    -------
    str
        The example document's raw markdown source, including its YAML
        frontmatter block.
    """
    return read_packaged_text("qa", "example")

"""Resource: specmgr://qa/template (Phase 4, Task 4.2).

Read-only, addressable counterpart of the ``get_qa_template`` tool, mirroring
this repo's existing tool+resource pairs (e.g. ``get_req_template`` /
``specmgr://req/template``) for a host that wants to fetch the template as
context without an explicit tool call. Deliberately does not import from
``qa.tools`` (nor vice versa): both this resource and the ``get_qa_template``
tool import the shared, doc-type-agnostic ``general.tools._packaged_data``
helper directly, so neither sub-package depends on the other just for this
one file read.

The resource's URI is deliberately unversioned (no ``/v1``), matching
``specmgr://qa/schema``/``specmgr://qa/example``'s own precedent.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from ...general.tools._packaged_data import read_packaged_text
from ...server import mcp


@mcp.resource(
    "specmgr://qa/template",
    name="qa_template",
    title="Question and Answer (QA) Template",
    description=(
        "A QA document template -- frontmatter and every body field present, populated with "
        "short placeholder ('blind text') content -- as raw markdown, for use as a starting "
        "point when drafting a new QA document."
    ),
    mime_type="text/markdown",
)
def qa_template() -> str:
    """Return the packaged QA template's full markdown text, verbatim.

    Same packaged-data source and no-cache, hard-failure-on-missing-file
    design as ``qa.tools.get_qa_template.get_qa_template`` -- this is
    simply that same read exposed as an MCP resource instead of a
    ``@mcp.tool()``. Not guaranteed to satisfy ``parse_qa``/``QaDocument``'s
    field-level validators -- see ``get_qa_template``'s own docstring.

    Returns
    -------
    str
        The template document's raw markdown source, including its YAML
        frontmatter block.
    """
    return read_packaged_text("qa", "template")

"""MCP resource registrations for Question and Answer (QA) documents (Phase 4, Task 4.2).

``qa_schema`` registers the persisted-JSON-Schema resource
(``specmgr://qa/schema``). ``qa_example`` registers the packaged sample QA
document resource (``specmgr://qa/example``). ``qa_template`` registers the
packaged QA template resource (``specmgr://qa/template``) -- every field
present, populated with short placeholder ("blind text") content rather
than a valid document instance. Import this package to register all QA
resources against the shared ``mcp`` application instance::

    from biz.dfch.specmgr.qa import resources  # noqa: F401 (side-effects only)

Like REQ, QA has no by-id single-document *resource* -- id-based reads go
through the ``get_qa`` tool only; see ADR
ddfb1109-422d-4507-8dbc-dc5e4bec9614 ("Expose id-based REQ document reads as
a tool (get_req), not a resource"). The former listing resource (``qa_list``,
``specmgr://qa/list``) was replaced the same way by the ``list_qa``
``@mcp.tool()`` (``qa.tools.list_qa``), so that paging parameters
(``max_results``/``offset``) could be accepted -- see
``.specmgr/feat/feat-13-list-paging/README.md``.
"""

from . import qa_example, qa_schema, qa_template  # noqa: F401

__all__ = [
    "qa_example",
    "qa_schema",
    "qa_template",
]

```

```
1: ---
2: created: 2026-08-18

8: ---
9: 
10: # Widget Registry Migration — Requirements Interview
11: 
12: ## General
13: 
14: ### Introduction
15: 
16: This Q&A session captures the requirements-elicitation interview backing the
17: widget-registry migration (see `tsk_reference.md`'s "Migrate Widgets to the
18: New Registry" task list). It was conducted across two sessions with the
19: platform team, plus one safety-reviewer sign-off session.
20: 
21: The transcript below is organized by ISO/IEC 25010:2023 quality
22: characteristic, with each answer optionally promoted to a concrete
23: `Requirement` callout wherever the interviewer judged that the answer implied
24: a concrete, testable requirement.
25: 
26: ### Raw Requirements
27: 
28: Prior to this interview, the following raw requirement notes already existed
29: on an internal wiki page and are preserved here verbatim for traceability:
30: the migration tool must be runnable from an engineer's laptop without VPN
31: access to the production registry cluster; it must never modify
32: WidgetRegistryV1 in any way; and it must produce a machine-readable migration
33: report suitable for attaching to the change-management ticket.
34: 
35: ## Functional Suitability
36: 
37: ### What must happen if a widget fails to migrate cleanly?
38: 
39: <!-- Elicited during the 2026-08-17 stakeholder workshop; flagged as safety-relevant. -->
40: 
41: #### Requirement
42: 
43: The system must roll back a partially migrated widget to its original
44: WidgetRegistryV1 registration if any step of the migration to
45: WidgetRegistryV2 fails, so no widget is left in an inconsistent, half-migrated
46: state.
47: 
48: Rollback should cover, at minimum:
49: 
50: - the widget's registration entry itself
51: 
52:   > Per the original design note: "the registration entry is the single
53:   > source of truth for a widget's active registry."
54: 
55: - any dependent configuration keys copied during migration
56: 
57: > Should the rollback also restore any listeners the widget had registered
58: > under WidgetRegistryV1, or is losing those listeners on failure an
59: > acceptable trade-off for now?
60: 
61: Losing listeners on failure is acceptable for v1 of the migration tool; they
62: can be re-registered manually. A follow-up ticket will track automating
63: listener rollback separately.
64: 
65: ### How should duplicate widget names be handled during migration?
66: 
67: > If two widgets end up with the same name after migration, should the tool
68: > halt entirely, or skip the duplicate and continue with a warning?
69: 
70: The tool should skip the duplicate, log a warning containing both widget
71: IDs, and continue; a manual reconciliation step happens after the bulk
72: migration completes.
73: 
74: ## Performance Efficiency
75: 
76: ### How fast must the migration script process the full widget inventory?
77: 
78: > Is a nightly batch run acceptable, or does this need to run within a
79: > maintenance window measured in minutes?
80: 
81: A maintenance-window constraint applies: the full inventory of roughly a
82: dozen widgets must migrate within 15 minutes to stay inside the currently
83: scheduled deployment window.
84: 
85: ## Compatibility
86: 
87: ## Interaction Capability
88: 
89: ### Does the migration tool need an interactive confirmation step?
90: 
91: > Should the operator running the migration see a confirmation prompt
92: > listing each widget before it proceeds, or is a fully unattended run
93: > acceptable?
94: 
95: An interactive confirmation prompt is required for the first production run;
96: unattended mode can be added later once the tool has proven itself in
97: staging.
98: 
99: ## Reliability
100: 
101: ### What happens if the registry service is unreachable mid-migration?
102: 
103: > Should the tool retry automatically, or fail immediately and require a
104: > manual restart?
105: 
106: The tool should retry with exponential backoff up to three attempts before
107: failing and requiring a manual restart.
108: 
109: ## Security
110: 
111: ### Who is authorized to run the migration against production?
112: 
113: > Is this restricted to the platform team, or can any engineer with deploy
114: > access run it?
115: 
116: Only members of the platform team may run the migration against production;
117: broader deploy access is not sufficient authorization on its own.
118: 
119: ## Maintainability
120: 
121: ### How should the migration script be structured for future reuse?
122: 
123: > Should this be a one-off script, or a reusable module other future
124: > registry migrations can call into?
125: 
126: It should be a reusable module, since at least one more registry migration
127: is already anticipated for next quarter.
128: 
129: ## Flexibility
130: 
131: ### Can the migration be re-run safely if it's interrupted?
132: 
133: > Is the migration idempotent, so re-running it after an interruption is
134: > safe, or does it require manual cleanup first?
135: 
136: The migration must be idempotent: re-running it against an
137: already-partially-migrated inventory should skip already-migrated widgets
138: and resume with the rest.
139: 
140: ## Safety
141: 
142: ### What is the fallback if WidgetRegistryV2 itself has an outage during the cutover?
143: 
144: <!-- Flagged by the safety reviewer during sign-off. -->
145: 
146: #### Requirement
147: 
148: The cutover procedure must keep WidgetRegistryV1 fully operational and
149: authoritative until WidgetRegistryV2 has confirmed at least one full
150: read/write cycle for every migrated widget, so a V2 outage during cutover
151: never leaves the system without a working registry.
152: 
153: > If WidgetRegistryV2 becomes unavailable partway through the cutover, does
154: > traffic automatically fall back to WidgetRegistryV1, or does an operator
155: > need to trigger that manually?
156: 
157: Traffic falls back to WidgetRegistryV1 automatically via the existing
158: feature-flag switch; no manual operator action is required, though the
159: on-call engineer is paged either way.
160: 
161: ## More Information
162: 
163: This document was produced as a scripted interview across the nine
164: ISO/IEC 25010:2023 quality characteristics, plus a general introduction and
165: a raw-requirements dump, ahead of formalizing the "Migrate Widgets to the
166: New Registry" task list (see `tsk_reference.md`). The `Compatibility`
167: category was intentionally left without any question/answer pairs for this
168: iteration, since the migration is entirely internal to the company's own
169: systems and raises no external interoperability or co-existence concerns
170: worth eliciting yet; it may be revisited if an external consumer of
171: WidgetRegistryV2's API is identified later.

(End of file - total 171 lines)
</content>
```

```
"""``schema`` -- generate JSON Schema (2020-12) for registered document-type models.

Generic, doc-type-agnostic command: each document type that wants a generated
JSON Schema artifact registers a ``generate_x() -> str`` function in
``_GENERATORS`` below, keyed by its short doc-type name (``"req"``, ``"uc"``).
``--type`` restricts generation to one registered type; omitting it generates
**all** registered types. Each type is written to its own
``{output_dir}/{type}_schema.json`` (default ``docs/``).

Unlike ``adr-toc``/``docs``, drift detection is built into this command
itself rather than left to a separate ``git diff --exit-code`` CI step: the
previous on-disk content (if any) is compared against the freshly generated
content for every type this invocation touches, and the command exits with
status 1 if any of them differ (including a file that did not exist yet).
The file is still (re)written either way, so a local run always leaves
``docs/`` up to date for a developer to commit; only the exit code signals
drift, which is what a CI step relies on directly.

The emitted dialect is Pydantic v2's native JSON Schema 2020-12 (``$defs``,
not ``definitions``) -- see `feat-6-requirement-artifact`'s README
"Decisions Made" for why this deliberately diverges from
``uc_schema.json``'s hand-authored draft-07.
"""

import json
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Annotated, Callable

import typer
from pydantic.json_schema import GenerateJsonSchema

from .._paths import DOCS_DIR
from ..qa.models.v1 import SCHEMA_COMMENT_VERSION as QA_SCHEMA_COMMENT_VERSION
from ..qa.models.v1.document import QaDocument
from ..req.models.v1 import SCHEMA_COMMENT_VERSION as REQ_SCHEMA_COMMENT_VERSION
from ..req.models.v1.document import ReqDocument
from ..tsk.models.v1 import SCHEMA_COMMENT_VERSION as TSK_SCHEMA_COMMENT_VERSION
from ..tsk.models.v1.document import TskDocument
from ..uc.models.v2 import SCHEMA_COMMENT_VERSION as UC_SCHEMA_COMMENT_VERSION
from ..uc.models.v2.document import UcDocument

_DEFAULT_OUTPUT_DIR = DOCS_DIR


def generate_req_schema() -> str:
    """Generate REQ's JSON Schema (2020-12 dialect) from ``ReqDocument.model_json_schema()``.

    Pydantic v2 deliberately omits the top-level ``$schema`` key by default
    (see ``GenerateJsonSchema.generate``'s own comment on this), so it is
    added explicitly here from ``GenerateJsonSchema.schema_dialect`` --
    otherwise the emitted file would not self-describe which JSON Schema
    dialect it actually uses.

    Also injects a ``"$comment"`` key holding
    ``req.models.v1.SCHEMA_COMMENT_VERSION`` (currently ``"v1"``) -- a bare
    schema-layout version token, distinct from any document instance's own
    ``frontmatter.version``, letting a caller that cached an earlier fetch
    detect a REQ schema shape change without diffing the whole file.

    Serializes with ``indent=2, sort_keys=True`` plus a trailing newline so
    repeated generation from unchanged models produces byte-identical
    output, which is what makes this command's own drift detection (and any
    downstream ``git diff``) meaningful.
    """
    schema_dict = ReqDocument.model_json_schema()
    schema_dict["$schema"] = GenerateJsonSchema.schema_dialect
    schema_dict["$comment"] = REQ_SCHEMA_COMMENT_VERSION
    return json.dumps(schema_dict, indent=2, sort_keys=True) + "\n"


def generate_qa_schema() -> str:
    """Generate QA's JSON Schema (2020-12 dialect) from ``QaDocument.model_json_schema()``.

    Mirrors :func:`generate_req_schema` exactly, but for ``qa.models.v1``:
    the ``"$schema"`` key is injected the same way (Pydantic v2 omits it by
    default), and ``"$comment"`` holds ``qa.models.v1.SCHEMA_COMMENT_VERSION``
    (currently ``"v1"``) instead of REQ's own version token.

    Serializes with ``indent=2, sort_keys=True`` plus a trailing newline, for
    the same byte-identical-output/drift-detection reason as
    :func:`generate_req_schema`.
    """
    schema_dict = QaDocument.model_json_schema()
    schema_dict["$schema"] = GenerateJsonSchema.schema_dialect
    schema_dict["$comment"] = QA_SCHEMA_COMMENT_VERSION
    return json.dumps(schema_dict, indent=2, sort_keys=True) + "\n"


def generate_uc_schema() -> str:
    """Generate UC's JSON Schema (2020-12 dialect) from ``UcDocument.model_json_schema()``.

    Mirrors :func:`generate_req_schema` exactly, but for ``uc.models.v2``:
    the ``"$schema"`` key is injected the same way (Pydantic v2 omits it by
    default), and ``"$comment"`` holds ``uc.models.v2.SCHEMA_COMMENT_VERSION``
    (currently ``"v2"``) instead of REQ's own version token.

    Serializes with ``indent=2, sort_keys=True`` plus a trailing newline, for
    the same byte-identical-output/drift-detection reason as
    :func:`generate_req_schema`.
    """
    schema_dict = UcDocument.model_json_schema()
    schema_dict["$schema"] = GenerateJsonSchema.schema_dialect
    schema_dict["$comment"] = UC_SCHEMA_COMMENT_VERSION
    return json.dumps(schema_dict, indent=2, sort_keys=True) + "\n"


def generate_tsk_schema() -> str:
    """Generate TSK's JSON Schema (2020-12 dialect) from ``TskDocument.model_json_schema()``.

    Mirrors :func:`generate_req_schema` exactly, but for ``tsk.models.v1``:
    the ``"$schema"`` key is injected the same way (Pydantic v2 omits it by
    default), and ``"$comment"`` holds ``tsk.models.v1.SCHEMA_COMMENT_VERSION``
    (currently ``"v1"``) instead of REQ's own version token.

    Serializes with ``indent=2, sort_keys=True`` plus a trailing newline, for
    the same byte-identical-output/drift-detection reason as
    :func:`generate_req_schema`.
    """
    schema_dict = TskDocument.model_json_schema()
    schema_dict["$schema"] = GenerateJsonSchema.schema_dialect
    schema_dict["$comment"] = TSK_SCHEMA_COMMENT_VERSION
    return json.dumps(schema_dict, indent=2, sort_keys=True) + "\n"


#: Registry mapping a doc-type name (as accepted by ``--type``) to its
#: ``generate_x() -> str`` function. Add an entry here when a new document
#: type's schema generator is implemented (e.g. ``"adr"``).
_GENERATORS: dict[str, Callable[[], str]] = {
    "qa": generate_qa_schema,
    "req": generate_req_schema,
    "tsk": generate_tsk_schema,
    "uc": generate_uc_schema,
}


def schema(
    type_: Annotated[
        str | None,
        typer.Option(
            "--type",
            help=f"Restrict generation to one registered doc type ({', '.join(sorted(_GENERATORS))}). "
            "Omit to generate all registered types.",
        ),

```

```
"""Shared, private constants for the ``uc.models.v2`` subpackage."""

from __future__ import annotations

#: The generated-schema layout version for ``docs/uc_schema.json``. Matches
#: this package's own folder name (``uc/models/v2``) -- bump only when a
#: breaking change to the *generated schema's* structure warrants a new
#: ``vN`` sibling package, not on every minor field addition. Consumed by
#: ``commands.schema.generate_uc_schema()`` as the emitted JSON's
#: ``"$comment"`` value, so a caller that cached an earlier fetch can detect
#: the schema changed shape without diffing the whole document. Deliberately
#: a bare token (``"v2"``, no ``"uc "`` prefix) -- the doc type is already
#: unambiguous from context (file name / resource URI).
SCHEMA_COMMENT_VERSION = "v2"
```

```
"""Tests for the `specmgr://qa/schema` resource (`qa.resources.qa_schema.qa_schema`)."""

import json
import tempfile
import unittest
from pathlib import Path
from unittest import mock

from biz.dfch.specmgr.general.tools import _packaged_data
from biz.dfch.specmgr.qa.resources.qa_schema import qa_schema


class TestQaSchemaResource(unittest.TestCase):
    """Tests for the `qa_schema` resource function."""

    def test_returns_parsed_repo_schema_by_default(self):
        """Against the real, committed packaged qa_schema.json, without any patching."""
        sut = qa_schema

        result = sut()

        self.assertIsInstance(result, dict)
        self.assertEqual(result["$comment"], "v1")
        self.assertEqual(result["$schema"], "https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema")
        self.assertIn("frontmatter", result["properties"])
        self.assertIn("body", result["properties"])

    def test_returns_parsed_dict_for_a_given_file(self):
        """A patched-in schema file's exact content must round-trip through json.loads."""
        payload = {"$schema": "https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema", "$comment": "v1", "properties": {}}
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
            schema_path = Path(tmp) / "qa_schema.json"
            schema_path.write_text(json.dumps(payload), encoding="utf-8")

            with mock.patch.object(_packaged_data, "packaged_data_path", return_value=schema_path):
                sut = qa_schema

                result = sut()

            self.assertEqual(result, payload)

    def test_reads_fresh_on_every_call(self):
        """No in-memory cache -- a second call must reflect an on-disk change since the first."""
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
            schema_path = Path(tmp) / "qa_schema.json"
            schema_path.write_text(json.dumps({"$comment": "v1"}), encoding="utf-8")

            with mock.patch.object(_packaged_data, "packaged_data_path", return_value=schema_path):
                sut = qa_schema

                first = sut()
                schema_path.write_text(json.dumps({"$comment": "v2"}), encoding="utf-8")
                second = sut()

            self.assertEqual(first["$comment"], "v1")
            self.assertEqual(second["$comment"], "v2")

    def test_raises_file_not_found_when_schema_missing(self):
        """A missing packaged qa_schema.json must propagate FileNotFoundError uncaught."""
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
            missing_path = Path(tmp) / "does-not-exist.json"

            with mock.patch.object(_packaged_data, "packaged_data_path", return_value=missing_path):
                sut = qa_schema

                with self.assertRaises(FileNotFoundError):
                    sut()

    def test_raises_json_decode_error_for_corrupted_file(self):
        """Corrupted (non-JSON) content on disk must propagate json.JSONDecodeError uncaught."""
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
            schema_path = Path(tmp) / "qa_schema.json"
            schema_path.write_text("{not valid json", encoding="utf-8")

            with mock.patch.object(_packaged_data, "packaged_data_path", return_value=schema_path):
                sut = qa_schema

                with self.assertRaises(json.JSONDecodeError):
                    sut()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

```

```
"""Tests for the `specmgr://qa/example` resource (`qa.resources.qa_example.qa_example`)."""

import tempfile
import unittest
from pathlib import Path
from unittest import mock

from biz.dfch.specmgr.general.tools import _packaged_data
from biz.dfch.specmgr.qa.resources.qa_example import qa_example
from biz.dfch.specmgr.qa.tools.get_qa_example import get_qa_example


class TestQaExampleResource(unittest.TestCase):
    """Tests for the qa_example resource function."""

    def test_returns_real_packaged_example(self):
        """Against the real, committed packaged data file, without any patching."""
        sut = qa_example

        result = sut()

        self.assertIsInstance(result, str)
        self.assertTrue(result.startswith("---\n"))
        self.assertIn("type: qa", result)
        self.assertIn("## General", result)

    def test_matches_the_get_qa_example_tool(self):
        """The resource and the tool must return identical content -- same underlying reader."""
        self.assertEqual(qa_example(), get_qa_example())

    def test_reads_fresh_on_every_call(self):
        """No in-memory cache -- a second call must reflect an on-disk change since the first."""
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
            example_path = Path(tmp) / "qa_example.md"
            example_path.write_text("first", encoding="utf-8")

            with mock.patch.object(_packaged_data, "packaged_data_path", return_value=example_path):
                sut = qa_example

                first = sut()
                example_path.write_text("second", encoding="utf-8")
                second = sut()

            self.assertEqual(first, "first")
            self.assertEqual(second, "second")

    def test_raises_file_not_found_when_example_missing(self):
        """A missing packaged example file must propagate FileNotFoundError uncaught."""
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
            missing_path = Path(tmp) / "does-not-exist.md"

            with mock.patch.object(_packaged_data, "packaged_data_path", return_value=missing_path):
                sut = qa_example

                with self.assertRaises(FileNotFoundError):
                    sut()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

"""Tests for the `specmgr://qa/template` resource (`qa.resources.qa_template.qa_template`)."""

import tempfile
import unittest
from pathlib import Path
from unittest import mock

from biz.dfch.specmgr.general.tools import _packaged_data
from biz.dfch.specmgr.qa.resources.qa_template import qa_template
from biz.dfch.specmgr.qa.tools.get_qa_template import get_qa_template


class TestQaTemplateResource(unittest.TestCase):
    """Tests for the qa_template resource function."""

    def test_returns_real_packaged_template(self):
        """Against the real, committed packaged data file, without any patching."""
        sut = qa_template

        result = sut()

        self.assertIsInstance(result, str)
        self.assertTrue(result.startswith("---\n"))
        self.assertIn("type: qa", result)
        self.assertIn("# Level 1 Heading is the Title of the Q&A Document", result)

    def test_matches_the_get_qa_template_tool(self):
        """The resource and the tool must return identical content -- same underlying reader."""
        self.assertEqual(qa_template(), get_qa_template())

    def test_reads_fresh_on_every_call(self):
        """No in-memory cache -- a second call must reflect an on-disk change since the first."""
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
            template_path = Path(tmp) / "qa_template.md"
            template_path.write_text("first", encoding="utf-8")

            with mock.patch.object(_packaged_data, "packaged_data_path", return_value=template_path):
                sut = qa_template

                first = sut()
                template_path.write_text("second", encoding="utf-8")
                second = sut()

            self.assertEqual(first, "first")
            self.assertEqual(second, "second")

    def test_raises_file_not_found_when_template_missing(self):
        """A missing packaged template file must propagate FileNotFoundError uncaught."""
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
            missing_path = Path(tmp) / "does-not-exist.md"

            with mock.patch.object(_packaged_data, "packaged_data_path", return_value=missing_path):
                sut = qa_template

                with self.assertRaises(FileNotFoundError):
                    sut()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

```

**Output:**
```
        ),
    ] = None,
    output_dir: Annotated[
        Path,
        typer.Option(
            "--output-dir",
            help="Directory to write '{type}_schema.json' files into (default: docs/).",
        ),
    ] = _DEFAULT_OUTPUT_DIR,
) -> None:
    """Generate JSON Schema (2020-12) for one or all registered document types.

    Writes ``{output_dir}/{type}_schema.json`` for each selected type
    (``--type``, or every registered type if omitted). Exits with status 1
    if any written file's content differs from what was already on disk
    (including the file not existing yet), so CI can rely on this command's
    own exit code instead of a separate ``git diff --exit-code`` step. The
    file is written regardless of drift, so a local run always leaves
    ``docs/`` up to date to commit.
    """
    if type_ is not None and type_ not in _GENERATORS:
        valid = ", ".join(sorted(_GENERATORS))
        typer.echo(f"Unknown --type {type_!r}; must be one of: {valid}")
        raise typer.Exit(1)

    selected = {type_: _GENERATORS[type_]} if type_ is not None else dict(_GENERATORS)

    output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    changed = False
    for name, generate in selected.items():
        output_path = output_dir / f"{name}_schema.json"
        new_content = generate()
        old_content = output_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8") if output_path.exists() else None

        output_path.write_text(new_content, encoding="utf-8")

        if old_content != new_content:
            changed = True
            typer.echo(f"✓ Wrote {output_path} (changed)")
        else:
            typer.echo(f"✓ Wrote {output_path} (unchanged)")

    if changed:
        raise typer.Exit(1)

```

```
1: ---

3: id: deaddead-feed-feed-feed-deaddeadfeed

5: type: qa
6: updated: 2026-08-18
7: version: 1.0.0
8: ---
9: 
10: # Widget Registry Migration — Requirements Interview
11: 
12: ## General
13: 
14: ### Introduction
15: 
16: This Q&A session captures the requirements-elicitation interview backing the
17: widget-registry migration (see `tsk_reference.md`'s "Migrate Widgets to the
18: New Registry" task list). It was conducted across two sessions with the
19: platform team, plus one safety-reviewer sign-off session.
20: 
21: The transcript below is organized by ISO/IEC 25010:2023 quality
22: characteristic, plus an `Elicitation Context` section describing who was
23: interviewed. Within each section, question/answer pairs appear directly one
24: after another, with no heading of their own.
25: 
26: ### Raw Requirements
27: 
28: Prior to this interview, the following raw requirement notes already existed
29: on an internal wiki page and are preserved here verbatim for traceability:
30: the migration tool must be runnable from an engineer's laptop without VPN
31: access to the production registry cluster; it must never modify
32: WidgetRegistryV1 in any way; and it must produce a machine-readable migration
33: report suitable for attaching to the change-management ticket.
34: 
35: ## Elicitation Context
36: 
37: <!-- Captures who was interviewed and the overall project context. -->
38: 
39: > Who participated in this interview series, and what prompted it?
40: 
41: The platform team (two engineers) participated across two sessions, plus a
42: dedicated safety-reviewer sign-off session focused specifically on the
43: cutover procedure. The interview was prompted by the upcoming deprecation of
44: WidgetRegistryV1, which forces every widget owner to migrate to
45: WidgetRegistryV2 ahead of the deprecation deadline.
46: 
47: ## Functional Suitability
48: 
49: <!-- Elicited during the 2026-08-17 stakeholder workshop; flagged as safety-relevant. -->
50: 
51: > What must happen if a widget fails to migrate cleanly, and should the
52: > rollback also restore any listeners the widget had registered under
53: > WidgetRegistryV1, or is losing those listeners on failure an acceptable
54: > trade-off for now?
55: 
56: The system must roll back a partially migrated widget to its original
57: WidgetRegistryV1 registration if any step of the migration to
58: WidgetRegistryV2 fails, so no widget is left in an inconsistent, half-migrated
59: state.
60: 
61: Rollback must cover, at minimum, the widget's registration entry itself (per
62: the original design note, "the registration entry is the single source of
63: truth for a widget's active registry") and any dependent configuration keys
64: copied during migration.
65: 
66: Losing listeners on failure is acceptable for v1 of the migration tool; they
67: can be re-registered manually. A follow-up ticket will track automating
68: listener rollback separately.
69: 
70: > If two widgets end up with the same name after migration, should the tool
71: > halt entirely, or skip the duplicate and continue with a warning?
72: 
73: The tool should skip the duplicate, log a warning containing both widget
74: IDs, and continue; a manual reconciliation step happens after the bulk
75: migration completes.
76: 
77: ## Performance Efficiency
78: 
79: > Is a nightly batch run acceptable, or does this need to run within a
80: > maintenance window measured in minutes?
81: 
82: A maintenance-window constraint applies: the full inventory of roughly a
83: dozen widgets must migrate within 15 minutes to stay inside the currently
84: scheduled deployment window.
85: 
86: ## Compatibility
87: 
88: ## Interaction Capability
89: 
90: > Should the operator running the migration see a confirmation prompt
91: > listing each widget before it proceeds, or is a fully unattended run
92: > acceptable?
93: 
94: An interactive confirmation prompt is required for the first production run;
95: unattended mode can be added later once the tool has proven itself in
96: staging.
97: 
98: ## Reliability
99: 
100: > Should the tool retry automatically, or fail immediately and require a
101: > manual restart?
102: 
103: The tool should retry with exponential backoff up to three attempts before
104: failing and requiring a manual restart.
105: 
106: ## Security
107: 
108: > Is this restricted to the platform team, or can any engineer with deploy
109: > access run it?
110: 
111: Only members of the platform team may run the migration against production;
112: broader deploy access is not sufficient authorization on its own.
113: 
114: ## Maintainability
115: 
116: > Should this be a one-off script, or a reusable module other future
117: > registry migrations can call into?
118: 
119: It should be a reusable module, since at least one more registry migration
120: is already anticipated for next quarter.
121: 
122: ## Flexibility
123: 
124: > Is the migration idempotent, so re-running it after an interruption is
125: > safe, or does it require manual cleanup first?
126: 
127: The migration must be idempotent: re-running it against an
128: already-partially-migrated inventory should skip already-migrated widgets
129: and resume with the rest.
130: 
131: ## Safety
132: 
133: <!-- Flagged by the safety reviewer during sign-off. -->
134: 
135: > What is the fallback if WidgetRegistryV2 itself has an outage during the
136: > cutover, and does traffic automatically fall back to WidgetRegistryV1, or
137: > does an operator need to trigger that manually?
138: 
139: The cutover procedure must keep WidgetRegistryV1 fully operational and
140: authoritative until WidgetRegistryV2 has confirmed at least one full
141: read/write cycle for every migrated widget, so a V2 outage during cutover
142: never leaves the system without a working registry.
143: 
144: Traffic falls back to WidgetRegistryV1 automatically via the existing
145: feature-flag switch; no manual operator action is required, though the
146: on-call engineer is paged either way.
147: 
148: ## More Information
149: 
150: This document was produced as a scripted interview across an `Elicitation Context` section plus the nine ISO/IEC 25010:2023 quality characteristics,
151: with a general introduction and a raw-requirements dump, ahead of
152: formalizing the "Migrate Widgets to the New Registry" task list (see
153: `tsk_reference.md`). The `Compatibility` category was intentionally left
154: without any question/answer pairs for this iteration, since the migration is
155: entirely internal to the company's own systems and raises no external
156: interoperability or co-existence concerns worth eliciting yet; it may be
157: revisited if an external consumer of WidgetRegistryV2's API is identified
158: later.

(End of file - total 158 lines)
</content>
```

```
"""``@mcp.prompt()``: create_qa (Phase 4, Task 4.3).

Returns instructional text -- not itself a tool call -- that guides an LLM
through drafting a brand-new Question and Answer (QA) document using the
existing ``qa/tools/``/``qa/resources/`` surface (``list_qa``,
``specmgr://qa/template``/``specmgr://qa/example``, ``specmgr://qa/schema``,
``create_qa``, ``validate_qa``). Structural shape ported 1:1 from
``req.prompts.create_req``, with the instructional content rewritten to
describe QA's own schema instead of REQ's.

Unlike ``adr.prompts.create_adr``, this prompt has no frontmatter-related
parameters to pre-fill: ``create_qa`` builds the entire QA frontmatter
itself (``id``/``type``/``status``/``created``/``updated``/``version``) --
the caller only ever supplies body markdown.

Naming note: this prompt is named ``create_qa``, the same name as the
``@mcp.tool()`` in ``qa/tools/create_qa.py``. This is not a collision --
the MCP protocol keeps prompts and tools in separate registries
(``prompts/list`` vs. ``tools/list``) -- but is called out here explicitly
so the two are not mistaken for the same registration.

The actual instructional text lives in its own packaged data file,
``qa/data/qa_create_instructions.md``, read fresh on every call via
``general.tools._packaged_data.read_packaged_text``, rather than as an
inline Python string constant. Placeholders use ``string.Template``
(``$topic``), not ``str.format``, precisely so the instructions file
itself is free to use plain, unescaped ``{...}`` braces for the QA
markdown headings it narrates to the LLM (e.g. ``# {title}``) without
those colliding with this module's own substitution.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from string import Template

from ...general.tools._packaged_data import read_packaged_text
from ...server import mcp


@mcp.prompt(
    name="create_qa",
    title="Create a QA document",
    description=(
        "Guides the LLM through checking for an existing similar QA document, gathering answers "
        "to ISO/IEC 25010:2023 characteristic-relevant questions, and driving "
        "create_qa/validate_qa to author a new QA document."
    ),
)
def create_qa(topic: str) -> str:
    """Return instructional text for drafting a new QA document about ``topic``.

    Parameters
    ----------
    topic:
        Free-text description of the interview's subject -- becomes the
        seed for the document's title and introduction.

    Returns
    -------
    str
        Instructional text (auto-wrapped as a single ``UserMessage`` by
        the MCP SDK), not itself a tool call.
    """
    template = Template(read_packaged_text("qa", "create_instructions", "md"))
    return template.substitute(topic=topic)

"""``@mcp.prompt()``: update_qa (Phase 4, Task 4.3).

Returns instructional text -- not itself a tool call -- that guides an LLM
through revising an existing Question and Answer (QA) document by id, using
the existing ``qa/tools/`` surface (``get_qa``, ``update_qa``,
``set_status_qa``, ``validate_qa``). Structural shape ported 1:1 from
``req.prompts.update_req``, with the instructional content rewritten to
describe QA's own schema and lifecycle instead of REQ's. Like ``get_req``,
step 1 points at the ``get_qa`` tool, not a ``specmgr://qa/{id}`` resource
-- there is no such resource; see ADR ddfb1109-422d-4507-8dbc-dc5e4bec9614.

Unlike ``adr.prompts.update_adr``, there is no ``update_frontmatter``/
``option_*`` equivalent here: QA's lifecycle surface is deliberately small
-- a whole-body replace (``update_qa``) plus a single, dedicated
status-change path (``set_status_qa``) -- so the tool-mapping section below
is correspondingly shorter.

The actual instructional text lives in its own packaged data file,
``qa/data/qa_update_instructions.md``, read fresh on every call via
``general.tools._packaged_data.read_packaged_text``, rather than as an
inline Python string constant. Placeholders use ``string.Template``
(``$id``/``$instructions``), not ``str.format``, precisely so the
instructions file itself is free to use plain, unescaped ``{...}`` braces
for the QA markdown headings it narrates to the LLM without those
colliding with this module's own substitution.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from string import Template

from ...general.tools._packaged_data import read_packaged_text
from ...server import mcp


@mcp.prompt(
    name="update_qa",
    title="Update a QA document",
    description=(
        "Guides the LLM through revising an existing QA document by id: reading current "
        "state, applying the requested change with the right tool, and validating."
    ),
)
def update_qa(id: str, instructions: str | None = None) -> str:
    """Return instructional text for revising the QA document identified by ``id``.

    Parameters
    ----------
    id:
        The existing document's specmgr-assigned identifier.
    instructions:
        Free-text description of the requested change. When absent, the
        returned instructions tell the LLM to ask the user first rather
        than guessing.

    Returns
    -------
    str
        Instructional text (auto-wrapped as a single ``UserMessage`` by
        the MCP SDK), not itself a tool call.
    """
    template = Template(read_packaged_text("qa", "update_instructions", "md"))
    return template.substitute(
        id=id,
        instructions=instructions or "(not given -- ask the user before making any change)",
    )

=== refine.py ===
# Copyright (C) 2026 Ronald Rink, d-fens GmbH, http://d-fens.ch
#
# This program is free software: you can redistribute it and/or modify
# it under the terms of the GNU Affero General Public License as published
# by the Free Software Foundation, either version 3 of the License, or
# (at your option) any later version.

# GNU Affero General Public License for more details.

# along with this program.  If not, see <https://www.gnu.org/licenses/>.
#
# SPDX-License-Identifier: AGPL-3.0-or-later

"""``@mcp.prompt()``: refine (Phase 4, Task 4.3).

Returns instructional text -- not itself a tool call -- that guides an LLM
through adding a fresh batch of open interview questions to an *existing*
Question and Answer (QA) document, one or more of the nine ISO/IEC
25010:2023 quality characteristics at a time (e.g. "5 questions each about
Functional Suitability, Security, Maintainability", or "3 questions for
each of the 9 main characteristics"). Unlike ``create_qa``/``update_qa``,
this prompt never elicits or writes an actual answer itself -- each new
question is appended with an empty ``_(awaiting response)_`` placeholder in
place of an answer, for a human to fill in directly in the document
afterwards.

Like ``update_qa``, this targets an existing document via the ``qa/tools/``
surface (``get_qa``, ``update_qa``, ``list_qa`` -- the last to resolve a
title to an id when no id is given) and the ``specmgr://iso25010`` resource
(to ground each new question in that characteristic's actual definition).
This prompt deliberately does not
implement or call any ``/resolve`` follow-up step itself -- it only tells
the human user, in its final instructions to the LLM, that such a step
exists and comes next.

The actual instructional text lives in its own packaged data file,
``qa/data/qa_refine_instructions.md``, read fresh on every call via
``general.tools._packaged_data.read_packaged_text`` -- the same
example/template/schema packaging convention this domain already uses
(``qa_example.md``/``qa_template.md``/``qa_schema.json``), rather than as
an inline Python string constant like every other prompt in this codebase.
Placeholders use ``string.Template`` (``$id_or_name``/``$scope``), not
``str.format``, precisely so the instructions file itself is free to use
plain, unescaped ``{...}`` braces for the Q&A markdown placeholders it
narrates to the LLM (e.g. ``{the question}``) without those colliding with
this module's own substitution.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from string import Template

from ...general.tools._packaged_data import read_packaged_text
from ...server import mcp


@mcp.prompt(
    name="refine",
    title="Add interview questions to a QA document",
    description=(
        "Guides the LLM through appending a batch of new, unanswered interview questions "
        "(each with an empty placeholder answer) to an existing QA document, for one or more "
        "of the nine ISO/IEC 25010:2023 quality characteristics."
    ),
)
def refine(id_or_name: str, scope: str | None = None) -> str:
    """Return instructional text for adding new interview questions to an existing QA document.

    Parameters
    ----------
    id_or_name:
        Either the target document's specmgr-assigned id, or a free-text
        title/topic to look up via the `list_qa` tool when no id is known.
    scope:
        Free-text description of which characteristics to target and how
        many questions to add to each, e.g. "5 questions each about
        Functional Suitability, Security, Maintainability" or "3 questions
        for each of the 9 main characteristics". When absent or ambiguous,
        the returned instructions tell the LLM to ask the user via the
        `question` tool rather than guessing.

    Returns
    -------
    str
        Instructional text (auto-wrapped as a single ``UserMessage`` by
        the MCP SDK), not itself a tool call.
    """
    template = Template(read_packaged_text("qa", "refine_instructions", "md"))
    return template.substitute(
        id_or_name=id_or_name,
        scope=scope or "(not given -- ask the user before choosing characteristics or counts)",
    )


```

```
43:   - the interviewee's free-form prose answer, as plain paragraphs
44:     immediately after the block quote (no heading of its own).
45:   All four of comment/requirement/question/answer are optional on every
```

```
"""Tests for the ``create_qa`` ``@mcp.prompt()``."""

import tempfile
import unittest
from pathlib import Path
from unittest import mock

from biz.dfch.specmgr.general.tools import _packaged_data
from biz.dfch.specmgr.qa.prompts.create_qa import create_qa


class TestCreateQaPrompt(unittest.TestCase):
    """Tests for the create_qa prompt."""

    def test_mentions_topic(self):
        """The topic argument must be interpolated into the returned text."""
        result = create_qa("Widget registry migration")
        self.assertIn("Widget registry migration", result)

    def test_mentions_duplicate_check_tool(self):
        """The prompt must instruct the LLM to check the list_qa tool first."""
        result = create_qa("Some topic")
        self.assertIn("list_qa", result)

    def test_mentions_starting_point_resources(self):
        """The prompt must point at the template/example/schema resources."""
        result = create_qa("Some topic")
        self.assertIn("specmgr://qa/template", result)
        self.assertIn("specmgr://qa/example", result)
        self.assertIn("specmgr://qa/schema", result)

    def test_mentions_tool_sequence_in_order(self):
        """The prompt must mention the list_qa tool, the template/example
        resources, specmgr://qa/schema, and create_qa, in that order,
        matching the intended sequence."""
        result = create_qa("Some topic")
        markers = [
            "list_qa",
            "specmgr://qa/template",
            "specmgr://qa/schema",
            "create_qa(content)",
        ]
        positions = [result.index(marker) for marker in markers]
        self.assertEqual(positions, sorted(positions))

    def test_mentions_all_nine_iso25010_characteristics(self):
        """All nine ISO/IEC 25010:2023 characteristic headings must be named."""
        result = create_qa("Some topic")
        for heading in (
            "Functional Suitability",
            "Performance Efficiency",
            "Compatibility",
            "Interaction Capability",
            "Reliability",
            "Security",
            "Maintainability",
            "Flexibility",
            "Safety",
        ):
            self.assertIn(heading, result)

    def test_mentions_general_and_more_information_sections(self):
        """The `General` and `More Information` sections must be named."""
        result = create_qa("Some topic")
        self.assertIn("## General", result)
        self.assertIn("### Introduction", result)
        self.assertIn("### Raw Requirements", result)
        self.assertIn("## More Information", result)

    def test_mentions_update_qa_for_later_revisions(self):
        """The prompt must point at the update_qa prompt for later changes."""
        result = create_qa("Some topic")
        self.assertIn("update_qa", result)

    def test_instructions_loaded_from_packaged_data_file(self):
        """The instructional text must come from qa/data/qa_create_instructions.md,
        not an inline Python string -- reads fresh on every call, no cache."""
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
            instructions_path = Path(tmp) / "qa_create_instructions.md"
            instructions_path.write_text("first $topic", encoding="utf-8")

            with mock.patch.object(_packaged_data, "packaged_data_path", return_value=instructions_path):
                first = create_qa("Some topic")
                instructions_path.write_text("second $topic", encoding="utf-8")
                second = create_qa("Some topic")

            self.assertEqual(first, "first Some topic")
            self.assertEqual(second, "second Some topic")

    def test_raises_file_not_found_when_instructions_missing(self):
        """A missing packaged instructions file must propagate FileNotFoundError uncaught."""
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
            missing_path = Path(tmp) / "does-not-exist.md"

            with mock.patch.object(_packaged_data, "packaged_data_path", return_value=missing_path):
                with self.assertRaises(FileNotFoundError):
                    create_qa("Some topic")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

```

```
"""Tests for the ``update_qa`` ``@mcp.prompt()``."""

import tempfile
import unittest
from pathlib import Path
from unittest import mock

from biz.dfch.specmgr.general.tools import _packaged_data
from biz.dfch.specmgr.qa.prompts.update_qa import update_qa


class TestUpdateQaPrompt(unittest.TestCase):
    """Tests for the update_qa prompt."""

    def test_mentions_id(self):
        """The id argument must be interpolated into the returned text."""
        result = update_qa("abc-123")
        self.assertIn("abc-123", result)

    def test_mentions_get_qa_tool_first(self):
        """The prompt must instruct the LLM to call get_qa first,
        before the update_qa write tool."""
        result = update_qa("abc-123")
        self.assertIn("get_qa(id)", result)
        self.assertLess(result.index("get_qa(id)"), result.index("update_qa(id, content)"))

    def test_mentions_both_mutation_tools(self):
        """Both update_qa and set_status_qa must be named."""
        result = update_qa("abc-123")
        for tool in ("update_qa", "set_status_qa"):
            self.assertIn(tool, result)

    def test_instructions_interpolated_when_given(self):
        """A given instructions string must appear verbatim in the returned text."""
        result = update_qa("abc-123", instructions="Change the status to done.")
        self.assertIn("Change the status to done.", result)

    def test_prompts_for_input_when_instructions_absent(self):
        """Absent instructions must tell the LLM to ask the user, not guess."""
        result = update_qa("abc-123")
        self.assertIn("ask the user", result)

    def test_mentions_whole_body_replace_warning(self):
        """The whole-body-replace caveat for update_qa must be present."""
        result = update_qa("abc-123")
        self.assertIn("whole-body replace", result)

    def test_mentions_status_never_via_update_qa(self):
        """The prompt must clarify that update_qa never changes status."""
        result = update_qa("abc-123")
        self.assertIn("update_qa` never accepts or changes `status`", result)

    def test_mentions_valid_status_values(self):
        """The four valid status values must be named."""
        result = update_qa("abc-123")
        for status in ("draft", "active", "done", "cancelled"):
            self.assertIn(status, result)

    def test_instructions_loaded_from_packaged_data_file(self):
        """The instructional text must come from qa/data/qa_update_instructions.md,
        not an inline Python string -- reads fresh on every call, no cache."""
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
            instructions_path = Path(tmp) / "qa_update_instructions.md"
            instructions_path.write_text("first $id / $instructions", encoding="utf-8")

            with mock.patch.object(_packaged_data, "packaged_data_path", return_value=instructions_path):
                first = update_qa("abc-123", instructions="Change the status to done.")
                instructions_path.write_text("second $id / $instructions", encoding="utf-8")
                second = update_qa("abc-123", instructions="Change the status to done.")

            self.assertEqual(first, "first abc-123 / Change the status to done.")
            self.assertEqual(second, "second abc-123 / Change the status to done.")

    def test_raises_file_not_found_when_instructions_missing(self):
        """A missing packaged instructions file must propagate FileNotFoundError uncaught."""
        with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:
            missing_path = Path(tmp) / "does-not-exist.md"

            with mock.patch.object(_packaged_data, "packaged_data_path", return_value=missing_path):
                with self.assertRaises(FileNotFoundError):
                    update_qa("abc-123")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    unittest.main()

```

```
1: You are drafting a new Question and Answer (QA) document about: $topic
2:

10: 
11: ## 0. Check for an existing QA document on this topic first

14: about it and ask whether they want to revise that one (via the
15: `update_qa` prompt) instead of creating a duplicate. Only proceed to
16: step 1 if this is genuinely a new interview.
17: 
18: ## 1. Structure recap (body markdown only, no frontmatter block)
19: - `# {title}` -- H1, mandatory, free-form.
20: - `## General` -- mandatory, always present.
21:   - `### Introduction` -- mandatory. Free-form prose framing the
22:     interview: who was interviewed, when, and why.
23:   - `### Raw Requirements` -- mandatory. Free-form, pre-existing raw
24:     requirement notes (e.g. from a wiki page), preserved verbatim for
25:     traceability. May simply note there were none.
26: - Ten fixed `##` category headings, each always present, in this exact
27:   order and exact wording: `## Elicitation Context` first, then the nine
28:   ISO/IEC 25010:2023 quality characteristics: `Functional Suitability`,
29:   `Performance Efficiency`, `Compatibility`, `Interaction Capability`,
30:   `Reliability`, `Security`, `Maintainability`, `Flexibility`, `Safety`.
31:   `## Elicitation Context` is QA-schema-specific -- unlike the other nine, it
32:   is **not** one of the ISO/IEC 25010:2023 quality characteristics; it
33:   captures context about the interview itself (stakeholders, scope, why
34:   it is happening) rather than a product-quality characteristic. Do not
35:   rename, reorder, or omit any of these ten headings -- a category with
36:   nothing to ask yet is still written as an empty heading with nothing
37:   under it.
38:   Under each category heading, add zero or more adjacent question/answer
39:   pairs, one directly after another, with **no heading of its own** for
40:   any pair. Each pair may optionally include, in this order:
41:   - an HTML comment (`<!-- ... -->`), giving context (e.g. when/by whom
42:     this was elicited) for the question that immediately follows it;
43:   - `> {the interviewer's question}` as a block quote;
44:   - the interviewee's free-form prose answer, as plain paragraphs
45:     immediately after the block quote.
46:   All three of comment/question/answer are optional on every Q&A pair --
47:   include whichever apply.
48: - `## More Information` -- optional freeform supplementary text (e.g.
49:   noting which category was deliberately left empty, and why).
50: 
51: ## 2. Gather information before calling any tool
52: Elicit (asking the user if not already given) the introduction/context,
53: any pre-existing raw requirements, then work through `Elicitation
54: Context` (general context about the interview itself) followed by the
55: nine ISO/IEC 25010:2023 categories one at a time, asking plausible
56: characteristic-relevant questions and recording the answers. Not every
57: category needs a question -- an internal-only change, for example, may
58: legitimately leave `Compatibility` empty.
59: 
60: ## 3. Use the template/example/schema as references
61: Fetch `specmgr://qa/template` or `specmgr://qa/example` as a starting
62: point/style reference, then check `specmgr://qa/schema` (the generated
63: JSON Schema) to confirm field names and constraints before drafting the
64: body. Do not invent field names or section headings that are not present
65: there, and do not rename or reorder the ten fixed category headings.
66: 
67: ## 4. Tool call sequence
68: 1. Draft the body-only markdown per the structure above.
69: 2. Call `create_qa(content)` -- `content` is body markdown only; the
70:    entire frontmatter is built automatically. A structural or field
71:    validation failure raises uncaught and nothing is written.
72: 3. Optionally call `validate_qa(content, full=False)` first if you want
73:    to dry-run the body without writing anything -- `create_qa` already
74:    performs the same validation internally, so this step is never
75:    required, only a convenience.
76: 
77: ## 5. Later revisions
78: Any later change to this QA document should go through the `update_qa`
79: prompt (or directly through `update_qa`/`set_status_qa`), not by
80: re-running this prompt.
```

```
+        self.assertNotIn("### {question-ish heading}", result)
+
+    def test_no_longer_mentions_requirement_callout(self):
+        """v1's `#### Requirement` callout has no v2 equivalent."""
+        result = create_qa("Some topic")
+        self.assertNotIn("#### Requirement", result)
+
     def test_mentions_update_qa_for_later_revisions(self):
         """The prompt must point at the update_qa prompt for later changes."""
         result = create_qa("Some topic")
diff --git a/tests/qa/prompts/test_refine.py b/tests/qa/prompts/test_refine.py
index 1cbf796..4761bc3 100644
--- a/tests/qa/prompts/test_refine.py
+++ b/tests/qa/prompts/test_refine.py
@@ -102,6 +102,18 @@ class TestRefinePrompt(unittest.TestCase):
         result = refine("abc-123")
         self.assertIn("Never touch", result)
 
+    def test_mentions_elicitation_context_as_selectable_target(self):
+        """The v2 `Elicitation Context` category must be offered as a
+        tenth selectable target alongside the nine characteristics."""
+        result = refine("abc-123")
+        self.assertIn("`Elicitation Context`", result)
+
+    def test_no_longer_mentions_per_pair_h3_heading(self):
+        """v1's per-question H3 heading phrasing must be gone in v2's
+        adjacent-pairs structure."""
+        result = refine("abc-123")
+        self.assertNotIn("### {question-ish heading}", result)
+
     def test_instructions_loaded_from_packaged_data_file(self):
         """The instructional text must come from qa/data/qa_refine_instructions.md,
         not an inline Python string -- reads fresh on every call, no cache."""

index c57e266..86b4a56 100644
--- a/tests/qa/prompts/test_update_qa.py
+++ b/tests/qa/prompts/test_update_qa.py
@@ -73,6 +73,16 @@ class TestUpdateQaPrompt(unittest.TestCase):
         for status in ("draft", "active", "done", "cancelled"):
             self.assertIn(status, result)
 
+    def test_mentions_elicitation_context(self):
+        """The v2 `Elicitation Context` category must be mentioned."""

+
+    def test_no_longer_lists_requirement_as_a_pair_field(self):
```

```
18: """MCP server for ``biz-dfch-specmgr``.
19: 
20: Requires the ``mcp`` extra (``pip install biz-dfch-specmgr[mcp]``).
21: 
22: Registers the following resources and tools so far (plan §8, §9a):
23: 
24: Resources
25: ---------
26: specmgr://version --    Installed version number of the ``biz-dfch-specmgr`` package.
27: specmgr://adr/{id} --    Full ADR document for a given id (``.specmgr/feat/feat-9-doc-in-specmgr/adr-tool-plan.md``).
28: specmgr://req/schema -- The generated REQ JSON Schema, read from a packaged data copy
29:                         (kept in sync with ``docs/req_schema.json``) so it works from a
30:                         real, non-editable install.
31: specmgr://req/example -- A complete, valid sample requirement document as raw markdown.
32: specmgr://req/template -- A requirement template (every field present, placeholder text)
33:                           as raw markdown.
34: specmgr://uc/schema --  The generated UC JSON Schema, read from a packaged data copy
35:                         (kept in sync with ``docs/uc_schema.json``) so it works from a
36:                         real, non-editable install.
37: specmgr://uc/example -- A complete, valid sample use case document as raw markdown.
38: specmgr://uc/template -- A use-case template (every field present, placeholder text)
39:                           as raw markdown.
40: specmgr://tsk/schema -- The generated TSK JSON Schema, read from a packaged data copy
41:                         (kept in sync with ``docs/tsk_schema.json``) so it works from a
42:                         real, non-editable install.
43: specmgr://tsk/example -- A complete, valid sample task list document as raw markdown.
44: specmgr://tsk/template -- A task list template (every field present, placeholder text)
45:                           as raw markdown.
46: specmgr://qa/schema --  The generated QA JSON Schema, read from a packaged data copy
47:                         (kept in sync with ``docs/qa_schema.json``) so it works from a
48:                         real, non-editable install.
49: specmgr://qa/example -- A complete, valid sample question-and-answer document as raw
50:                         markdown.
51: specmgr://qa/template -- A question-and-answer template (every field present,
52:                           placeholder text) as raw markdown.
53: specmgr://iso25010 --   The ISO/IEC 25010:2023 product quality model's nine main
54:                         characteristics (and sub-characteristics), each with a description.
55: 
56: REQ has no ``specmgr://req/{id}`` resource, unlike ADR -- id-based reads go
57: through the ``get_req`` tool only (ADR ddfb1109-422d-4507-8dbc-dc5e4bec9614).
58: UC has no ``specmgr://uc/{id}`` resource either, for the same reason -- id-based
59: reads go through the ``get_uc`` tool only. TSK has no ``specmgr://tsk/{id}``
60: resource either -- id-based reads go through the ``get_tsk`` tool only, and
61: there never was such a resource to remove in the first place. QA has no
62: ``specmgr://qa/{id}`` resource either, for the same reason -- id-based reads go
63: through the ``get_qa`` tool only.
64: 
65: Tools
66: -----
67: ADR tools (``adr/tools/``): ``get_adr``, ``list_adr``, ``create_adr``, ``update_frontmatter``,
68: ``update_section``, ``set_status``, ``option_list``, ``option_create``,
69: ``option_update``, ``option_read``, ``option_delete``, ``validate_adr``.
70: Use-case tools (``uc/tools/``): ``parse_uc``, ``get_uc``, ``list_uc``, ``get_uc_example``,
71: ``get_uc_template``, ``create_uc``, ``update_uc``, ``set_status_uc``, ``delete_uc``
72: (stub, not yet implemented), ``validate_uc``.
73: Requirement tools (``req/tools/``): ``parse_req``, ``get_req``, ``list_req``, ``get_req_example``,
74: ``get_req_template``, ``create_req``, ``update_req``, ``set_status_req``, ``delete_req``
75: (stub, not yet implemented), ``validate_req``.
76: Task list tools (``tsk/tools/``): ``parse_tsk``, ``get_tsk``, ``list_tsk``, ``get_tsk_example``,
77: ``get_tsk_template``, ``create_tsk``, ``update_tsk``, ``set_status_tsk``, ``delete_tsk``
78: (stub, not yet implemented), ``validate_tsk``.
79: QA tools (``qa/tools/``): ``parse_qa``, ``get_qa``, ``list_qa``, ``get_qa_example``,
80: ``get_qa_template``, ``create_qa``, ``update_qa``, ``set_status_qa``, ``delete_qa``
81: (stub, not yet implemented), ``validate_qa``.
82: General tools (``general/tools/``): ``mdformat`` -- format markdown files in place,
83: preserving YAML frontmatter blocks; ``webfetch`` -- fetch a URL over HTTP GET with a
84: bearer token, restricted to a configured base URL (``SPECMGR_WEBFETCH_BASE_URL``,
85: ``SPECMGR_WEBFETCH_BEARER``).
86: 
87: Prompts
88: -------
89: ADR prompts (``adr/prompts/``): ``create_adr``, ``update_adr`` -- instructional
90: text guiding an LLM through the ADR tool sequence above (``.specmgr/feat/feat-9-doc-in-specmgr/adr-tool-plan.md``
91: text guiding an LLM through the ADR tool sequence above (``.specmgr/feat/feat-9-doc-in-specmgr/adr-tool-plan.md``
92: §11).
93: Requirement prompts (``req/prompts/``): ``create_req``, ``update_req`` --
94: instructional text guiding an LLM through the REQ tool sequence above (Task 3.19).
95: Task list prompts (``tsk/prompts/``): ``create_task``, ``update_task`` -- instructional
96: text guiding an LLM through the TSK tool sequence above, plus ``implement_task`` --
97: reads an existing task list via ``get_tsk``, builds a ``TodoWrite`` list from its
98: items, and uses the ``question`` tool to resolve ambiguity before proceeding.
99: QA prompts (``qa/prompts/``): ``create_qa``, ``update_qa``, plus ``refine`` --
100: appends a fresh batch of unanswered interview questions (each with an empty
101: `_(awaiting response)_` placeholder) to an existing QA document, for one or
102: more of the nine ISO/IEC 25010:2023 quality characteristics.
103: General prompts (``general/prompts/``): ``compact_history`` -- guides rotating
104: older ``### Recent Updates`` entries out of any `.specmgr` feature folder's
105: ``README.md`` into an optional sibling ``history.md``, per ADR
106: e369ee2e-3353-4f92-991c-6367d76d832e.
107: 
108: Modules are grouped domain-first
109: (ADR ece4554b-725c-4f76-bc04-5d2b760363d2: "Organize the codebase by
110: document-type domain"): each document
111: domain (``adr``, ``uc``, ``req``, ``tsk``, ``qa``, and later ``ac``) is a top-level package
112: with its own ``tools``/``prompts``/``resources`` sub-packages, self-
113: registered via the domain package's own ``__init__.py``. Cross-cutting, non-domain-specific
114: tools/resources/prompts (e.g. ``specmgr://version``/``specmgr://iso25010`` resources,
115: the ``mdformat`` tool, or the ``compact_history`` prompt) stay under the top-level
116: ``general`` package instead (``general.tools``/``general.resources``/``general.prompts``).
117: Add a new domain by
118: creating its top-level package and importing it at the bottom of this
119: module, next to the existing ``adr``/``general``/``qa``/``req``/``tsk``/``uc``
120: imports, so its ``@mcp.tool()`` / ``@mcp.prompt()`` / ``@mcp.resource()``
121: decorators actually run. ``req``, ``tsk``, and ``qa`` each register ``tools``,
122: ``resources``, and ``prompts``; ``general`` now also registers all three; ``uc``
123: registers ``tools`` and ``resources`` only -- it has no ``prompts`` sub-package yet.
124: """
125: 
126: from __future__ import annotations
127: 
128: from collections.abc import AsyncGenerator
129: from contextlib import asynccontextmanager
130: 
131: from mcp.server import MCPServer
132: 
133: 
134: @asynccontextmanager
135: async def _lifespan(_server: MCPServer) -> AsyncGenerator[None, None]:
136:     """Placeholder lifespan: no shared state to initialise yet."""
137:     yield
138: 
139: 
140: mcp = MCPServer(
141:     name="specmgr",
142:     instructions="An artifact manager for system specifications.",
143:     lifespan=_lifespan,
144: )
145: 
146: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
147: # Resource/tool/prompt registration (side-effect: registers everything on
148: # mcp). Every domain package here (including the cross-cutting `general`
149: # package, which in turn imports its own `resources`/`tools` sub-packages)
150: # must be imported for its @mcp.tool()/@mcp.prompt()/@mcp.resource()
151: # decorators to actually run.
152: # ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
153: 
154: from . import adr, general, qa, req, tsk, uc  # noqa: E402, F401

(End of file - total 154 lines)
</content>
```
