**Output:**
```
# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.0.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

## [0.8.0] - 2026-08-19

### Changed

- **BREAKING**: Removed the five `specmgr://<domain>/list` MCP resources
  (`adr`, `req`, `uc`, `tsk`, `qa`); replaced by paged `list_<domain>`
  `@mcp.tool()`s (`list_adr`, `list_req`, `list_uc`, `list_tsk`, `list_qa`)
  accepting `max_results`/`offset` and returning a shared `PagedResult`
  wrapper (`total`, `offset`, `max_results`, `truncated`, `results`).
  Resources cannot accept parameters, so pagination required the
  resource→tool conversion (see ADR
  ec9f5262-9912-49d0-903f-fcfb54f28c13). Callers must switch from
  `resources/read --uri specmgr://<domain>/list` to `tools/call
  --tool-name list_<domain>`.

### Added

- Shared `general/models/PagedResult[T]` and `general/tools/_paging`
  (`normalize_paging`/`paginate`) infrastructure, and a shared
  `general/models/DocSummary` base for four of the five domains' summary
  models (`ReqSummary`/`UcSummary`/`TskSummary`/`QaSummary`); `AdrSummary`
  stays a deliberate, documented outlier (field-identical, not a
  subclass, since `models/adr` must stay free of the `mcp` extra).

## [0.7.0] - 2026-08-18

### Added

- **`compact_history` MCP prompt** (`general/prompts/`): New prompt for compacting
```

## Assistant (Phase-Orchestrator · Claude Sonnet 5 · 15.2s)

_Thinking:_



No, `1.0.0` is correct there — this is a subtle but important distinction, and your question hits exactly the point that caused Phase 3's blocker.

**Two different "versions" are in play, and only one of them is `version:` in the frontmatter:**

1. **`frontmatter.version`** (the field you're asking about) — this is the shared `models.md` **parsing engine's** own schema version (`MarkdownFrontmatter.version`), hardcoded to major `1` (`SCHEMA_MAJOR_VERSION = 1` in `models/md/_util.py`). It's shared identically across *every* document type — ADR, REQ, TSK, UC, QA — and only changes if the frontmatter block's own shape (the YAML keys like `id`/`status`/`created`/`updated`/`version` themselves) ever breaks. It has never changed, so every document type's frontmatter always says `version: 1.0.0`, regardless of that domain's own body schema.

2. **QA's own "v1" vs "v2" body schema** (`qa/models/v1/` vs `qa/models/v2/` Python packages) — this is a *code-organization* distinction, not something recorded anywhere inside the document itself. There's no frontmatter field that says "this document's body follows the v2 shape." The only way to know which body schema a document was written for is: does its markdown actually have a `## Elicitation Context` section and adjacent Q&A pairs (v2), or one `### {heading}` per pair (v1)? The Python package that parses it (now exclusively `qa/models/v2/`, since Phase 4's cutover) is what enforces that shape.

**Confirmed by existing precedent** — I just checked `uc_example.md`, which is exactly this situation already: UC went through the identical v1→v2 body-schema migration in an earlier feature, and its packaged example still carries `version: 1.0.0` today, not `2.0.0`. Same for every other domain's example.

This is precisely the misunderstanding the original plan (REQ-004) baked in — it assumed `version`'s major component could be bumped to "2" to signal QA-v2-ness, and that turned out to be structurally impossible (`QaFrontmatter.model_validate({"version": "2.0.0", ...})` raises `ValidationError` outright). That's the blocker I escalated to you, and your Option-1 decision (mirror UC's no-gate cutover) is exactly what makes `version: 1.0.0` the *correct* value here, not a leftover oversight.

So: no bug — `qa_example.md`/`qa_template.md` correctly keep `version: 1.0.0`, and that's intentional, tested (`test_qa_schema.py`/Phase 3's Decisions Made explicitly call this out), and consistent with every other domain in the repo.
